# Running the WiCAN tests

One entry point: **`.\test.ps1`** (PowerShell, repo root). Bench details and
inventory live in `components/TESTBENCH.md`. `.\test.ps1 list` prints every
runnable suite/check.

**Two ESP-IDF installs since 2026-10-27** (v6.1 beside v6.0.2): `build.ps1`
and `test.ps1` take `-Idf 6.0.2|6.1` (default 6.1 since 6.0.0-beta.5, the
first release built with it; 6.0.2 stays installed) and set the matching
IDF tree and Python environment themselves (v6.1's own export.ps1 does not
find the installer's venv). Switching the IDF needs a fresh `build\`: delete
it, since `-Clean` would run the other IDF's fullclean on a foreign cache.
The first v6.1 build (2026-10-27) compiled clean (the 13 warnings are the
vendored components' and predate it: same compiler, same flags), re-resolved
`dependencies.lock` (idf 6.1.0, the managed components unchanged) and added
the 6.1 symbols to `sdkconfig`; the image grew about 25 KB (25 % of the app
partition free).
Release Test PASS 59/59 on it; the one measurable change is internal RAM: the
settled free after boot is 3.9 KB higher, the boot's floor 10 KB lower (39.7 KB),
set inside the HTTP server's start. The boot map prints `WICAN RAMMIN <step>=<min>`
since that day (the internal minimum at the end of every step that lowered it,
then `end=`), beside `WICAN RAMMAP` (the retained delta per step) and
`WICAN BOOTTIME`: a step that borrows RAM for a moment shows in RAMMIN only.
The Release Test's `supply_current_awake_band` (90..300 mA) assumes the ESPNetLink
on the DUT's USB port; with the PC cable there instead the PSU reads 88 to 91 mA.

**Every run writes a dated summary report to `test-reports\`** (results
table, performance numbers, DUT conditions, git revision): that folder is
the pass/fail + performance history; see `test-reports/README.md` for how to
compare runs. Raw per-stage logs go to `test-reports\logs\<run>\` (local
only). Suppress with `-NoReport`.

**One WICAN_TEST_AP on the air** (since 2026-10-02 the bench AP is the P4
`bench_ap` on its NCM link, NetworkManager profile `wican-bench-usb`; the
Pi's own hotspot profiles `wican-bench` and `wican-bench-w0` stay parked).
Two radios with that SSID make the DUT roam between them and drop every TCP
connection on each move: the 6.0.0-beta.5 release run (2026-10-27) paid two
WiFi drops and six MQTT error lines because a Pi reboot had re-armed the
twins through the Pi's pre-P4 boot service and the hotspot watchdog bounced
the internal radio under the DUT. The Pi scripts in `tools/testbench/pi`
park the twins while the P4 serves since that night (deploy with
`deploy_bench_pi.sh` on the Pi), and the bench preflight prints which radio
serves (`ap=P4 (wican-bench-usb)`) and refuses a run with a twin beside the
P4, naming the parking command. A DUT log reading `STA got IP 10.42.0.x` or
a bssid starting 2c:cf:67 means the twin is up.

**Every test directory has a `README.md` stating exactly what it covers and
what the expected passing output is** (Unity summary for host suites, ordered
serial markers for target apps, pytest verdict for HIL):

- `components/<comp>/host_test/README.md`: host unit suites (×28)
- `components/<comp>/test_apps/README.md`: self-contained on-target apps (×12)
- `components/wifi_manager/test_apps_hil/README.md`: the WiFi HIL suite

## The test kinds

| Command | What runs | Needs |
|---|---|---|
| `.\test.ps1 check` | **The quick bench check**: only the bench preflight below (~10 s), then `RESULT: PASS/FAIL`. Run it after re-plugging anything: it names the console port it found (`serial=COM254 (CH344 B)`), the DUT address it resolved, and the FTDI (PSU) / `MeatPi USB-CAN` (ECU sim) ports, and since 2026-10-27 which radio serves WICAN_TEST_AP (`ap=P4 (wican-bench-usb)`; a Pi hotspot twin beside the P4 fails the preflight with the parking command) | PC + rpi001 |
| `.\test.ps1 host [component]` | Host **unit** suites (`components/*/host_test`, 526 tests across 44 components as of 2026-07-26; the summary prints a per-suite and TOTAL count) on the bench Pi's IDF linux target, syncs sources automatically. Optional component name runs just that suite. **Pi offline:** `bash tools/testbench/host_gcc_wsl.sh <vpn_manager\|config_backup>` from WSL compiles that PURE suite with the system gcc + Unity under ASan/UBSan (no IDF build; 2026-10-16, issues #985 to #989: `vpn_manager` 11, `config_backup` 24; add a case per IDF-free suite) | `ssh rpi001` reachable (the WSL form: gcc in WSL, the IDF tree on C:) |
| `.\test.ps1 target <component>` | Builds + flashes that component's **self-contained on-target app** (`components/<comp>/test_apps`), captures serial, verifies `TEST DONE` with no crash signatures. `ble_manager` is special: waits for `BLE READY`, then drives the DUT from rpi001's BLE controller (`ble_bench.py`) and passes on `BLE BENCH PASS`. `restart_tracker` is special too (2026-10-05): its app crashes the DUT on purpose, one step per boot (15 steps, about a minute), and `tools/testbench/system/rt_target_check.py` judges every crash note against IDF's own panic text of the same crash (`RT TARGET PASS`, 85 checks since the evening of 2026-10-05: the same 13 crashes, all quick and all different, also prove the crash-loop brake and the stored crash report on the device's own RTC memory and NVS: the streak counts every one, the verdict turns to `park` at the third, the report is stored four times and not a fifth, and the app clears it at the end); run it at every IDF version bump. Since the components live in their own repository a test app's `..\..\..\sdkconfig.defaults` and partition table do not exist there: `test.ps1` stages copies from this repo for the build and removes them (no target app had been built since the split until 2026-10-05; the other apps still build every component of the tree, `restart_tracker`'s and `filesystem`'s use `MINIMAL_BUILD`). `filesystem`'s app carries the 32 GB card of issue #982 since 2026-10-11 (`INFO_SD`: a link-time wrap of `esp_vfs_fat_info` stands in for the card, 1,897,503 clusters of 16 KiB, one used; a failed check withholds `TEST DONE`, so the stage goes red): before the fix it printed `total=1023918080 ... ok=0` and `TEST FAILED checks=1` on the bench unit (`test-reports/2026-10-11_2005_target_filesystem.md`); with `filesystem_info()` in `uint64_t` the same day: `ok=1`, `TEST DONE`, 0 E lines, `RESULT: PASS` in 16.4 s (`2026-10-11_2028_target_filesystem.md`). The bench unit's own card is the field-faithful check: a 16 GB SanDisk SC16G that `/api/fs/info?path=/sd` reported as 3,038,806,016 B (2.83 GB on the File Manager) for three months and reads as 15,923,707,904 B (14.83 GB) since, equal to `/api/backup/status` `free_bytes` + used; `test-reports/logs/issue982_20261011/files_page_real.mjs` reads the page in Chrome through a tunnel and prints AGREES / DISAGREES against that sum. **Flash the main firmware back afterwards** (`.\build.ps1 -Flash COMx`) | DUT console port (the preflight picks it; + rpi001 for ble_manager) |
| `.\test.ps1 hil` | **WiFi hardware-in-the-loop** pytest: real STA connect / reconnect / fallback / ban / AP-auto-disable against the Pi's AP (add `-Flash` to rebuild+flash the HIL app first) | DUT + `ssh rpi001` |
| `.\test.ps1 live` | **Live checks vs the composed main firmware**: CLI over WebSocket (`cli_ws_test.py` → `CLI WS PASS`; incl. `system -t` task monitor and, since 2026-10-11, the dispatcher's verdict lines: `bogus` → `Unknown command`, `led -c` → `command failed (1)`; the `Internal error` verdict of issue #980 is host-tested only, a healthy device cannot produce it) + OBD over WebSocket (`ws_live_test.py` → `WS LIVE PASS`; it pauses/restores every autopid group via `POST /api/autopid/group`: autopid co-masters the chip and its traffic fans out to `/ws/obd`; since 2026-10-21 it sends a warm-up CR and drains it before judging `ATI`: with autopid enabled the firmware yields the chip to the first app command by aborting the poll, and the chip's `STOPPED` for that abort lands on that command, which apps simply retry) + real-DBC parser cross-check (`dbc_real_test.py` → `DBC REAL PASS`; uploads the fixture DBCs, diffs every signal against the host reference) + the USB-Ethernet / espnetlink benches (auto-**SKIP** when hardware absent) + **network-trust lockdown sweep LAST** (`wifi_gate_live_test.py` → `WIFI GATE PASS`: flips `sta_trusted` false, probes EVERY `/api` route × 4 methods + UI + WS over the STA address expecting 403/405 only, USB admin + MQTT must stay up; reboots the DUT twice, restores). Needs the canonical bench settings (below) | main fw on DUT at `-DutIp` + rpi001 |
| `.\test.ps1 blesec` | **BLE bonding + security end-to-end** (`ble_security_test.py` → `BLE SECURITY PASS`). BLE sits on the internal-RAM cliff with the full stack, so it auto-switches the DUT to **WiFi-off / BLE-on** (~53 KB free), runs three scenarios on the Pi's UB500 dongle: **gate** (a WRONG passkey can't pair, so no characteristic is readable), **pair** (the correct passkey pairs via MITM and the gated Device-Info read works), **persist** (after a DUT reboot the bonded central reconnects with **no** passkey: NVS bond persistence), then restores `apsta` + BLE-off. Reboots the DUT ~3×; leaves it on WiFi. Runs on the PC (USB link + ssh to the Pi) | main fw on DUT + rpi001 + UB500 |
| `.\test.ps1 perf` | **Performance battery vs the composed main**, every scenario's numbers land in the report: MQTT RTT (512B) + paced 4000B drain (`mqtt_bench.py`, needs mqtt enabled against the Pi broker; the device silently ignores `size > 4000` = `BENCH_MAX_SIZE`), WS echo latency via `/ws/can` + OBD-poll via `/ws/obd` (`ws_bench.py`), BLE A/B RTT (`ble_ab.py`, runs **last**: a BLE connect makes interface_manager suspend WiFi, which also skips its TCP throughput legs by design; clean-radio BLE TX = manual `ble_blast.py`) | main fw on DUT + rpi001 |
| `.\test.ps1 usbeth` | **USB-Ethernet bench** (`usb_eth_bench.py` → `USB ETH TARGET PASS`): adapter status, HTTP + throughput over the usb-eth netif, reboot re-enumeration The wire may land on either Pi shared profile (`eth0` `eth-bench` 10.42.1.x, or - since 2026-08-26 - a USB-Ethernet adapter on the Pi as `eth1`, profile `eth-bench-usb` 10.42.2.1/24); the script accepts any `10.42.` lease by default, an optional 2nd arg pins the prefix. The DUT address (1st arg) takes a comma-separated candidate list: BOTH Pi hotspots broadcast `WICAN_TEST_AP` (wint0 10.42.0.x, wtest0 10.42.1.x) and the DUT may re-join either after the reboot leg (`10.42.0.194,10.42.1.194` for the 68ee8f5a653d unit; the .62 pin belongs to the other unit). Re-run 2026-08-26 (AX88772B on the WiCAN, cdc_ncm adapter on the Pi, espnetlink build v4.51p_beta): PASS 10/10, HTTP sample 392 KB/s, attaches=1 after reboot; iperf2 over the wire TCP TX 6.45 / RX 7.71 Mbit/s, UDP `-b 20` 7.00 Mbit/s 0% loss 1.68 ms jitter (matches the 2026-07-13 baseline in `iperf_manager/README.md`) | main fw on DUT + the adapter on the USB connector + Pi `eth-bench`/`eth-bench-usb` profile |
| `.\test.ps1 espnetlink` | **ESPNetLink LTE-dongle bench** (`espnetlink_bench.py` → `ESPNETLINK TARGET PASS`): the RNDIS data path (enum + IP) AND the CDC-ACM management console (`ver`, `lte -s` signal/operator/PPP, carrier IP). `--prove-lte` (manual) isolates the Pi's WiFi internet and confirms SNTP over the modem. **Since 2026-08-24 the default `espnetlink.mode=wifi_modem` CUTS the dongle's USB data after pairing**: this USB-data bench needs `espnetlink.mode=usb_ncm`/`usb_rndis` (or `auto_pair=false`) + `usb_acm_cli.enabled`. **2026-08-25: three selectable transports** (`wifi_modem`/`usb_ncm`/`usb_rndis`, the pairing pass sets the dongle's `usb_dev_ethernet.class` to match, one dongle reboot). Bench-verified full cycle: wifi_modem steady (cut, GPS from power-on) -> usb_ncm (the AP-path `usb_data` restore un-cuts the boot-cut dongle, `driver cdc_ncm`, uplink espnetlink_usb, GPS + SNTP over the wire) -> usb_rndis (class flip + re-enum as RNDIS, `driver rndis`, GPS + SNTP) -> wifi_modem (key re-read, cut, fix survives); the WiFi-modem path is verified from the console instead: boot log `identified … → credentials ok → usb gone → STA got IP 192.168.80.x → uplink: none -> espnetlink (AP)`, `espnetlink` → `gps: valid=1`, `rtc -s` syncs via the dongle, `espnetlink repair` cuts again with 0 reboots / 0 `E` lines (see `components/espnetlink_link/README.md`). **2026-10-12: the default is `usb_rndis` now and nothing is cut (the dongle's `wifi_modem` persistence is gone: it starts with its USB data on at every boot and cuts only when the WiCAN asks, until the next boot; `console` is the recovery mode), so this bench runs on a default pair. The bench takes the DUT address as its first argument and the stage passes its resolved one (it had 10.42.0.62 hard-coded and failed on the Pi with `Network is unreachable`); PASS 15:06 on the kick build (`test-reports/2026-10-12_1506_espnetlink.md`). 2026-10-18 02:38 `ESPNETLINK TARGET PASS` on the release pair (dongle `v1.22-61-gcc81591`): driver rndis, data-path 192.168.7.2, ACM `ver`, modem attached, PPP connected, signal, a carrier address, 7 s; the same session ran the dongle's new `factory selftest`, `gps -V`, `lte -s` and `wifi_modem` through `POST /api/usb/acm/cmd` (the self-test as one 930-byte JSON line, under the bridge's 2 KB cap). 2026-10-22 23:49 `ESPNETLINK TARGET PASS` on the production unit `288485bb38f1` as shipped on 1.23.0 against the released 6.0.0-beta.2, and the `factory selftest` line clean (`internal/TASK_espnetlink_release_test.md` section 5); 2026-10-23 11:04 `ESPNETLINK TARGET PASS` on the older unit 894AA5 on 1.23.0 against the fixed build v6.0.0p-beta.2-1-g8432a50, and 18:42 on the production unit against the final build with the engine's `unreachable` state (a run chained 20 s after the OTA gate's last dongle restart fails the four `lte -s` checks while the LTE engine is still starting: give a restarted dongle its LTE first)** | main fw on DUT + the espnetlink on the USB connector (usb_host_manager + usb_acm_cli enabled) |
| `.\test.ps1 stackaudit` | **Stack + memory audit, both halves** (2026-07-22, born from the silent PSRAM-stack overflow): (1) *static*, `tools/stack_audit.py` reads the compiler's per-function frame sizes (`-fstack-usage` is permanently on; `.su` files land next to every `.obj`) and cross-references them against every `xTaskCreate*` stack size (PSRAM vs internal detected), flagging frames > 40% of a same-component task stack for human triage; (2) *runtime*: `stack_audit_test.py` on the Pi exercises the deep paths (DTC scan job, test-a-PID), then asserts EVERY live task's `stack_hw` >= 512 B (warn < 1024), the ephemeral job tasks' exit-log watermarks (`dtc job stack_hw=` / `std scan stack_hw=` via `/api/logs/ring`), and heap floors on BOTH placements (internal min_free >= 20 KB + largest_block >= 16 KB, PSRAM min_free >= 1 MB). The runtime half also runs inside `live` | static: local build; runtime: main fw on DUT + rpi001 |
| `.\test.ps1 sleep` | **Sleep-mode HIL** (`sleep_bench_test.py` → `SLEEP BENCH PASS`, ~14 min; **2026-10-12 21:31 `SLEEP BENCH PASS` with the ESPNetLink on the connector in `usb_rndis` on the rework image**, run as `sleep_bench_test.py --dut-ip localhost:18081` through an ssh tunnel to the Pi because this PC had lost its route to the bench subnet (the stage's direct run failed its first step for that reason alone); 4 planned wakes, the E lines only the esp-mqtt pair of each boot): OWON PSU walks the 12 V input across the sleep/wake thresholds; entry proven by supply current, wake by `/api/restart/history`. Since 2026-10-01 (sleep_manager v3, `wake_mv`) a **wake-voltage leg**: sleep 12.9 V / wake 13.4 V with a 1 min delay, 12.5 V = sleep entry, then 13.2 V held 40 s (between the two) must NOT wake the device (supply current stays at the nap level, HTTP dead), 13.6 V must (`power_wake` from `sleep_mode` in the restart history); the restore puts 13.1 / 13.2 V and the 5 min delay back. On a firmware without `wake_mv` the leg is skipped with a note. The **wake hold** (`wake_delay_ms`, v4) is timed from the DEVICE console (the bench opens COM254: seconds between `state: wake_pending` and `waking by reboot (voltage recovered)`; the supply current cannot time it, its own detection latency is ~8 s): the default 0.5 s hold must read 0.3 to 2.6 s, the 5 s hold 4.5 to 7.6 s and at least 3 s more. **2026-10-01: PASS** (entry 63 s, 151 -> 49 mA, default hold 1.24 s, 5 s hold 5.24 s, 13.2 V held 40 s asleep, 13.6 V `power_wake`; the 8 E lines were all esp-mqtt's connect-failure class). The first run of the night FAILED and found the sleep-entry panic with the USB host active (see the brief's traps; CherryUSB deinit fix). **Critical-floor leg** (Ali, 2026-10-01): sleep DISABLED in settings, 11.75 V on the supply: still answering HTTP at ~60 s, asleep within ~120 s of the floor (console line `critical battery: ... under 11.90 V for 120 s`), stays asleep, 14 V wakes it as `power_wake`. **2026-10-01 22:54 run: every sleep/wake check PASS incl. the floor (asleep 128 s after the 11.75 V drop with sleep disabled, device line at 120 s, stays asleep, `power_wake`); the two FAILs were the bench's own single HTTP probe at 60 s and the final restore read on a flaky hotspot moment (the restore had applied), both hardened since** **2026-10-02 08:51, first run over the `bench_ap` P4 link: every network step clean (every wake reboot rejoined the P4 AP, no discovery hiccup), all sleep/wake/hold checks PASS; the floor leg failed as "no entry in 210 s" and the device's own console explained it: the DUT still carried the Tailscale config the matrix's betty scenario had left (control server unreachable), vpn_manager restarted it mid-leg and the firmware PANICKED (Cache error in `lwip_netconn_do_dns_found` <- `dns_tmr`: microlink_stop() waited a fixed 3 s and destroy freed a task stack still blocked in getaddrinfo), the reboot reset the floor's timer; the boot-loop guard then slept the device by itself (3 unexpected resets under 12.1 V). Fix: a real task join in microlink_stop() (2026-10-02)** **The sleep countdown (2026-10-06, after a default device slept 2 min into a Quick Setup on an 11.6 V supply: the critical floor cutting the 5 min delay short, with "Sleep after 5 min" on the page and nothing saying a sleep was coming).** `/api/sleep` carries `pending` (`none` / `delay` / `critical`: the rule that will put the device to sleep first) and `sleep_in_s`, the `sleep` command prints `sleeps in N s (...)`, and the bench holds both against the device in every leg: `countdown_idle` at 14 V (none, 0, the floor's 11.9 V and 120 s); in the ladder leg the countdown names the delay of the settings, runs at one second per second by HTTP and by the console (`ladder_countdown_pace`: two `sleep` commands 10 s apart, stamped by the console reader) and is cleared by the recovery; in the voltage leg the entry must come when the countdown said (`voltage_countdown_kept`: the read's promise against the device's own `entering sleep` line, within 2.5 s); with sleep disabled the floor is the only countdown (`state` normal, `pending` critical) and keeps its promise; and **leg 5, the floor ahead of the default delay** (as it was while the floor was 2 min; see the end of this row for its 5 min form): default settings again, 11.6 V: `state` low_voltage with `pending` critical and at most 120 s, the console's `sleeps in N s (critical floor: under 11.90 V for 120 s)`, asleep at the floor and not at 5 min, the device's `critical battery` line, the promise kept, `power_wake` at 14 V. **2026-10-06 03:15: PASS** (840 s; the three promises off by +0.1 s, -0.1 s and -0.6 s; pace 279 s -> 268 s over 10.40 s on the console; leg 5: `sleep_in_s` 115 at the first read, asleep 135 s after the supply step at 48 mA; 9 restart records, all planned; the 8 E lines are esp-mqtt's connect-failure class after the wake boots, as on 2026-10-01). The run before it FAILED two of these checks by 3 to 4 s, and the cause was the bench's own clock: the ssh hop to the Pi takes 0.3 s as a rule and 3 to 7 s after an idle pause, the HTTP exchange sits at its END, and the read was stamped at the middle of the call. `get_timed()` stamps a read with curl's own `time_total` now, and a read whose exchange took over a second is taken again. The same night the firmware went from publishing seconds (up to one task pass old) to publishing the deadline, counted at the read. Also by hand that night: the DUT's own page in Chrome through a tunnel at 12.5 V showed `Going to sleep in 5:00.` counting in step with `/api/sleep` (299, 298, ... s), in Quick Setup too. **The floor is 5 min since 2026-10-06 09:00 (Ali: "under 11.9 V change it from 2 to 5 min"; `SM_CRITICAL_DELAY_MS` 300000, pinned by the host suite).** The legs time the floor by the device's own `critical_s` (`countdown_idle` requires 300): the critical-floor leg waits out 5 min with sleep disabled and adds `floor_not_at_2_min` (HTTP answers later than 150 s under the floor, where the old rule had the device asleep); leg 5 is now **a default device under the floor**: default settings, 11.6 V, a countdown that starts at 5 min and names a rule (the floor when the two deadlines tie, the sleep delay when the ladder saw the falling supply a sample earlier), the `sleep` command agreeing, `default_floor_not_at_2_min`, asleep at 5 min, the promise kept, `power_wake`. The bench takes about 20 min now. **2026-10-06 09:11: PASS** (1180 s; sleep disabled at 11.75 V: 32 answers later than 150 s, asleep after 316 s, `critical battery: 11.69 V under 11.90 V for 300 s`, promise off by +0.1 s; default settings at 11.6 V: `pending critical`, 294 s at the first read, `sleeps in 281 s (critical floor: under 11.90 V for 300 s)`, 3 answers later than 150 s, asleep after 314 s at 48 mA, the floor fired, promise off by -0.7 s; the 1 min delay leg off by -0.2 s; 9 planned restarts, the same 8 esp-mqtt E lines). This run was on a DUT erased and restored that morning (the fresh-device fault hunt, NEW_FEATURE_BRIEF section 7). **The hold (Ali, 2026-10-06 11:00, "prompt the user to extend if he needs more time", decided from a mockup: 60 s before, 10 min a press, the floor may be held, 3 per boot, Let it sleep only closes, button on the bar and in the dialog):** `POST /api/sleep/hold {"minutes":N}` moves the sleep delay and the floor out to now + N min (pure `sm_hold_apply()`, under the sleep task's new mutex), three per boot. The bench presses it in the 1 min leg with about 30 s left: three answers 200 (the deadline a minute after each press, the answer's `hold_s` 60 and `holds_left` 0 after the third), the fourth 409 `hold limit reached`, the console's `held awake 1 min on request (3 of 3 this boot): sleeps in 60 s`, the `sleep` command's `held awake on request: 56 s left, 0 of 3 holds left this boot`, the entry a minute after the last press (`hold_countdown_kept`) and the timing judged from that press; and once under the floor with sleep disabled (`floor_hold_applied`: 200, `pending critical`, `state normal`, 60 s; `floor_hold_kept`). **2026-10-06 11:18: PASS** (1230 s; holds `[200, 200, 200, 409]`, the entry 60.8 s after the last press (off by +0.8 s), the floor's hold 61.0 s (off by +1.0 s), the floor leg asleep after 326 s with the hold, the default device at 11.6 V unchanged: 293 s on the countdown, asleep after 314 s, off by +0.1 s; 9 planned restarts, the same 8 esp-mqtt E lines) | PSU (the FTDI port, COM50 today) + rpi001 |
| `bash tools/testbench/pi/bench_ap_net.sh up` (on rpi001) | **bench_ap: the bench's home WiFi as an instrument** (2026-10-02, `tools/testbench/instruments/bench_ap/`): an ESP32-P4-Function-EV board on the Pi's USB, WPA2 soft-AP on its ESP32-C6 (esp_hosted) bridged raw at layer 2 to a USB CDC-NCM link; the Pi shares 10.42.1.0/24 on `usb0` (DHCP, mDNS, broker unchanged), both mt76/brcm hotspots parked, `dnsmasq-wtest0.leases` shimmed onto the NCM file so every bench's discovery works unchanged, a console daemon logs associations to `/tmp/bench_ap.console.log` and relays `ap`, `ap off|on`, `ap kick <mac>`, `ap channel`, `ap set`. Replaces the stalling mt76 hotspot (it cost 13 of 17 matrix scenarios twice). Pass bar: a client gets a lease from the Pi through the bridge, the DUT answers `/api/sleep`, kick and off/on cycle the station. **2026-10-02 bring-up: PASS** (wtest1 10.42.1.244, DUT 10.42.1.194 rssi -47, kick back in 10 s, off/on back in 5 s, 0 dropped frames). Known: the C6 slave firmware is old (host 2.12.0 vs 0.0.0, upgrade pending), the board is pre-3.0 P4 silicon (own build config). **2026-10-02 evening: the first image leaked every frame it forwarded from a station** (`usb_tx_done` freed the wrong argument) and asserted in esp_hosted after about 330 KB of station traffic (nine resets that day, each leaving the DUT in a zombie association until `ap off` / `ap on`; every AutoPID matrix run died in leg 5). Reproduced with 20 downloads of the DUT's log ring, fixed, re-flashed: 6.5 MB through with the heap flat, then three full matrix runs. `ap` prints `heap: free / min / largest` now: look there before blaming the DUT. Build/flash: `instruments/bench_ap/build.ps1` and the README (stop the console daemon, flash from the Pi, `bench_ap_net.sh up`) | rpi001 + the P4 board (both USB ports) |

| `.\test.ps1 autobaud` | **CAN listen before talk, auto bit rate and the autopid bus guard** (`tools/testbench/can/can_autobaud_bench.py` → `CAN AUTOBAUD PASS`, ~9 min, 18 DUT restarts, 2 simulator restarts; `TASK_j1939_wwh.md` phase 2). The claim: the DUT never destroys the traffic of a bus it cannot read, with its native controller or through the OBD chip, and it finds the bit rate of a live bus by listening. The JUDGE is the wire: the PCAN adapter keeps the bus alive (29-bit id 18FF1021, 200 frames/s), the ECU simulator at the same bit rate is the ACK source, and the adapter counts the bus errors it sees across every restart and in an 8 s steady window (PCAN reports a destroyed frame as one bus error plus eight counter-change messages with id 0: only the first kind counts). 18 legs: `n1`..`n4` on a 500k bus (500 normal runs; 250 normal and 250 silent read `mismatch` and destroy nothing; `auto` detects 500), `n5` the bus moves to 250k under the running auto node (demoted, re-detected), `n6`..`n8` on 250k (500 normal / silent: `mismatch`, nothing destroyed; 250 normal runs clean), `n9` auto on a silent bus stays listen-only until traffic names the bit rate, `n10` a fixed bit rate on a silent bus runs as configured, is demoted when traffic at another bit rate starts and stays listen-only after, `n11` auto + silent reads the adapter at every bit rate of the setting (125, 1000, 100, 83.3, 95.2, 33.3, 500, 250), `n12` traffic at 800k: every candidate tried, state `mismatch`, no bit rate claimed, `n13` the same bouncing node with `GET /api/can` asked from four threads for a minute: no reset (until 2026-10-05 a status read on a node the link policy was deleting loaded through a NULL register pointer, about one panic in 150 s of this: the reset of the EU truck bench's first run, TASK phase 7 addendum, finding 28); then the chip: `c0` 500k live with protocol Automatic polls, `c1` 250k live with protocol 6 pinned is parked before the first request (zero bytes to the chip, detection / std scan / test-a-PID / DTC scan / DTC clear answer 409 with the guard's sentence), `c2` Automatic with a stored protocol 6 on a 250k bus uses the chip's search and the store learns 8, `c3` silent at boot then 250k traffic: parked at the first look that reads frames, `c4` silent at boot then 800k traffic: parked as unreadable, polling resumes when it stops. Restores the simulator, the vehicle store's protocol and every setting; fails on an unexpected reset or an `E` line of its own tags (`/api/logs/ring`). `--only n5,c3` runs chosen legs (verdict `CAN AUTOBAUD PARTIAL PASS`). The stage opens its own ssh tunnel to the DUT (port 18081); by hand: `python tools/testbench/can/can_autobaud_bench.py localhost:8081`. **2026-10-03: PASS** (93 checks; demotion cost 4 and 5 frames in `n5` and `n10`, re-detection 73 ms, detection from silence 74 ms, chip parked 231 ms after the first 250k frame at a cost of 0, parked on unreadable traffic after 1.2 s; 0 unexpected resets). The first gate run that day FAILED with thousands of bus errors in every 250k leg and found that a DUT restart did not reset the OBD chip (it kept an open 500k session; fixed in `obd_chip_bringup.c`). Bench facts: the simulator cannot leave the bus (its CAN controller survives its software restart and keeps ACKing at its last bit rate, `enabled=false` or not), so the legs at other bit rates run with the DUT listen-only. **2026-10-03, the interrupt watchdog**: two of five runs of the day rebooted the DUT in `n11` (the walk reaching 83 / 95 kbit/s; `no_unexpected_reset` and `n11_reads_95k` failed; the first of them had been filed as a deep-sleep wake by restart_tracker's IDF 4 reset numbers); reproduced with the console tailed on the third `--only n11` run: `Interrupt wdt timeout on CPU0`, `_xt_lowint1` busy for 300 ms over an idle task = a TWAI bus-error interrupt storm (a 1000 kbit/s candidate listening to a slow bus: a bit error every few bit times, `rx_bad` +134 989 in 4 s) starving the tick. Fixed in `can_core_driver.c` (the error ISR masks the controller's interrupts past 20 000 errors/s, the link policy bounces the deaf node); `n11` ran four times clean with the console on afterwards, 10 storms throttled, the leg's `rx_bad` down to +1300; the bench asserts `n11_error_storms_throttled` since (`rx_storms` on `/api/can`). **2026-10-05: PASS** (95 checks, 533 s; `n13`: 2855 status reads against 207 bounces of the node). **2026-10-17 02:57: PASS** (497 s, `n13`: 3904 status reads against 205 bounces) on the build that added `can_ab_adopt()` (a tester's stated bitrate on a silent `auto` bus, see the `j2534auto` row): the policy's existing verdicts unchanged | PCAN on the DUT's bus + the ECU simulator (USB-NCM 192.168.8.1) + rpi001 (tunnel) |
| `.\test.ps1 profilee2e` (= `python tools/testbench/obd/autopid_profile_e2e_bench.py --dut localhost:18081 [--profiles FILE\|URL] [--car "Leaf ZE1 (2018-)"] [--watch 60] [--seed N]`; by hand through your own tunnel; `--profiles <checkout>\vehicle_profiles.json` tests a profile before it is pushed) | **A published vehicle profile end to end** (prints `AUTOPID PROFILE E2E PASS`, 38 checks, ~5 min, two planned restarts; 2026-10-21, the Nissan Leaf ZE1 (2018-) profile, the first one with passive CAN `filters` in the published file). The profile is imported the way the web UI imports it (`autopid_profile_bench.convert`: the expressions as published (no index shift since 2026-10-24), the renames, the filters with their frame id read like `Number()`), its init staged as `specific_init` and its name as `vehicle`, and polled against an ECU played FROM THE PROFILE on the PCAN: `actors/pcan_profile_ecu.py` answers every request the profile sends (ISO 15765-2 single and multi-frame, the tester's flow control honoured) with reply bytes the bench chose (seeded, inside every published min/max) and broadcasts every filter frame with bytes the bench chose; the ECU simulator stays on the bus as the ACK source and answers the standard rows found on the DUT. Claims: every request reached the ECU and was answered (no flow-control timeout), every filter parameter equals what its expression means on the frame data, every PID parameter equals what its expression means in the legacy frame of reference the published file uses (every byte after the CAN header, PCI bytes included: `parse_elm327_response()` in the legacy main/autopid.c), the rows keep being re-read, the standard rows keep reading the simulator beside the profile, then every PID through the test route (the chip's raw reply lines go to the log) and restore (tables, settings, `0100` answered by the simulator). A parameter past the 256-byte frames cap (`AP_FRAMES_MAX`) is reported as a warning, not failed. **First runs 2026-10-21 (firmware alfa.7-2-gb0fca3a): the three filters PASS** (SPEED, the signed DM_F_RPM and ODOMETER decoded exactly from 0x284 / 0x1DA / 0x5C5 broadcast every 50 ms), every request answered 20 times in 60 s, flow control 80 of 80, `polls_failed` +0, the standard rows kept reading RPM 800, restore clean; **the four multi-frame PIDs FAIL and the two single-frame ones pass**, two defects: (1) the importer shifts every index down by one, right for a single frame (`0L 41 05 xx`) and wrong for a multi-frame reply, whose published indexes count the first frame's two PCI bytes and one per consecutive frame while the chip hands v6 the PCI-free rows (`037 / 0: 61 01 .. / 1: ..`): 21018, 2103 and 21045 decode exactly the shifted expression on the PCI-free payload; (2) a reply over 16 rows is assembled in the wrong order: the chip prints row indexes 0..F and wraps, `assemble_headerless()` orders the rows by that printed index, so rows 16 and up interleave with 0 and up: the 28-row 2102 decodes exactly the shifted expression on that re-ordered payload (54 of 54 polled values, 84 of 84 from the test route; the Toyota bZ4X `22181A2` and the Xpeng `221122` are the two other published replies over 16 rows). **Fixed 2026-10-24 (issue #1014, ConorIA's Kia Niro EV was the field confirmation; Ali's option 2, `internal/TASK_profile_frame_ref.md` section 6): autopid reads the chip with headers on, as v4 did, and the expressions index the frames as the chip prints them (`ap_resp_frames()`), so the published profiles go in verbatim; the standard rows of a stored config are re-numbered at load.** RAN on the fixed build: `AUTOPID PROFILE E2E PASS`, 38 checks, 0 warnings: the four multi-frame PIDs decode as the profile means in the poll AND through the test route (21018 5/5, the 28-frame 2102 96/96 with no parameter past the cap any more, 2103 2/2, 21045 4/4), the two single-frame ones too, the three filters as before, the standard rows re-numbered at load read RPM 800 beside the profile, restore clean (the bench's last check expects the headers-on frame `7E8 06 41 00 ..` now). By hand the same day: `--car "Niro EV"` PASS (29 checks, 2201019 20/20, 2201057 5/5; the ECU simulator answers a 7E4 request with `7F 22 31` on 7EC, silence it per DID through `/api/ecu/errors` first, see the bench docstring), `--car bZ4X` PASS with the published hint 2 of `22181A2` removed in a local copy (28 frames 96/96; the hint stops the chip after two frames, a profile defect for main), `--car Xpeng` PASS with `ATFCSD300000` added in a local copy (221122 192/192 over 28 frames; the published init sets `ATFCSM1` without flow-control data, a profile defect for main). A profile that names no receive filter gets request + 8 as its reply id in the bench since 2026-10-24 | PC + PCAN_USBBUS2 on the DUT bus + the ECU simulator; the DUT on the bench AP, the PSU above the sleep voltage |

| `.\test.ps1 wwh` | **OBD over UDS (ISO 27145 WWH-OBD, SAE J1979-2) as an autopid dialect** (`tools/testbench/obd/wwh_obd_bench.py` → `WWH OBD PASS`, ~7 min, 12 DUT restarts, 8 simulator restarts; `TASK_j1939_wwh.md` phase 3). The claim: such a vehicle is detected, polled, its trouble codes read and cleared with no step an OBD-II car would not need, and the device tells the two kinds apart by itself when it is moved between them. The VEHICLE is the PCAN adapter (`actors/pcan_wwh_ecu.py`, real ISO-TP, one `ECU {json}` log line per request: which ECU, functional or physical, what it answered), so half of the checks are the wire's word; the ECU simulator stays on the bus as the ACK source with its own ECU off, and is the OBD-II car of the last leg. Ten legs: `w1` a vehicle the store has never seen (29-bit, 500k, two ECUs) is found by first contact with nobody asking: protocol 7, `dialect` uds, VIN, both ECUs with their bitmaps, 17 rows each addressed to its owner (`init: ATSH18DA00F1`, F43C to `3D`), expressions one byte further in; `w2` every value equals what the actor sends, each row is asked physically of its owner and nobody else is asked, no failed poll; `w3` a custom row with no init between the standard rows goes out with the FUNCTIONAL header (both ECUs see it); `w4` DTC scan: codes of both ECUs with status and severity, the lamp per ECU, the permanent code, `7F 19 78` ridden out twice per answer, the clear refused (403) with nothing sent while `dtc_allow_clear` is off, polling undisturbed; `w5` after a restart the known vehicle is back without a detection, the clear sends `14 FF FF 33` to both ECUs, codes gone, lamp off, the permanent code stays; `w6` the same codes in the SAE J1939 DTC format (`SPN3226-4`); `w7` an 11-bit vehicle with one ECU and no F810: protocol 6, rows addressed to `7E0`; `w8` the Mercedes Sprinter VS30 of the field report (`--sprinter`: ECUs 58 / 59 / 5D with the reported bitmaps, 763 rpm from 58): no row for a PID that decodes nothing, ECU `5A` turns up later with an unscaled copy of the engine speed and is never asked, the next boot meets four ECUs and it is still the same van; `w9` a 250k bus that is live at boot: the bus guard names the bitrate, the vehicle is found on protocol 9 and the adapter sees ZERO bus errors; `w10` back on the OBD-II simulator with nobody asking, then that car on 29-bit ids: its ECU is told apart (`18DAF110`) and a DTC scan reads it (it found no ECU before 2026-10-03). Restores the store (the bench's vehicles forgotten), the tables, every setting and the simulator; fails on an unexpected reset, a new fault or an `E` line of its own tags. After every job, before every restart and after every leg it also reads what the device only says in passing: the stack headroom the detection job, the vehicle writer and the DTC job log when they end, the poller's from the task list and the internal heap's floor (METRIC lines; under 512 B or under 20 KB fails, 2026-10-03: detection job 2948 B, vehicle writer 3108 B, poller 9368 B, DTC job 14200 B, heap floor 66895 B). `--only w4,w5` runs chosen legs after `w1` (verdict `WWH OBD PARTIAL PASS`). The stage opens its own ssh tunnel to the DUT (port 18081) and keeps the actor's logs in `test-reports/logs/`; by hand: `python tools/testbench/obd/wwh_obd_bench.py localhost:8081`. **2026-10-03: PASS** (59 checks; first value of a new vehicle 17.6 s after boot, of a known one 10.5 s, on the live 250k bus 17.8 s, after the switch back to the OBD-II car 13.7 s; 16.5 polls a second on 17 rows at a 1 s period). Traps: the simulator's CAN controller survives its restart, so `enabled=false` keeps it on the bus at the bitrate it LAST ran at (the 250k leg goes through `250k, on` first); never kill the actor (the PEAK driver wedges): the bench asks it to stop through `--stop-file`. Since 2026-10-09 the rows a detection stores are OFF (Ali: nothing polls until the user enables the standard PIDs and ticks rows): w1 checks `w1_rows_stored_off_until_ticked` and ticks them (`w1_rows_ticked_on`) before its values leg |

The truck is the PCAN actor (`actors/pcan_j1939_truck.py`, codec `lib/j1939_ref.py`, cross-checked against a J1939 DBC); the ECU simulator only acknowledges (its own ECU off, `enabled=false` after a restart AT the test bitrate). It found the store's pick moving on a silent bus and the look-time stamps (TASK phase 5 findings 6, 7). **Fourth stage (2026-10-03, phase 6): `tools/testbench/can/j1939_active_bench.py` → `J1939 ACTIVE PASS`** (~190.6 s, 5 restarts; the J2534 TCP port tunnelled as the diagnostics-hold instrument): the truck (PCAN, one active and two previously active codes) runs before the DUT boots with `j1939.mode active`; `a1` the address claim as the truck saw it (one Address Claimed from 249 with the device NAME), `claimed`, the TX counters; `a2` asking: a single-frame answer, the VIN by BAM, DM2 by RTS/CTS with our CTS / end-of-message, a NACK counted; `a3` the truck asks us for our address (answered) and for a group we lack (NACKed, naming it); `a4` a lesser NAME contesting our address is kept and defended once per round (not a storm), a better one moves us to 250; `a5` 1000 priority-0 frames/s while 60 requests go out: all answered, retries counted, nothing lost; `a6` autopid: an OBD-II car stored, the truck becomes the current car by its VIN (asked for) in ~6 s, a `?` row asks at its period and publishes, a refused `?` row is held, DM2 in the DTC report as pending items, the DM11 / DM3 clear acknowledged and the report empty after it; `a7` a J2534 tester attached: the asking stops, the reading goes on, released it resumes; `a8` listen mode: not one frame, a request 409; `a9` active on a listen-only bus: a listener that says so once. **2026-10-03: PASS** (57 checks; 8 retries for 60 frames under the flood, 2 of 20 lost before the arbitration budget; the car switch 6.0 s). It found the IDF node driver keeping the frame pointer, the single retry budget, the defence storm and the car-to-truck switch that never came (TASK phase 6 findings 13 to 16). For each, the NEXT boot's record in `/api/restart/history` must carry the note: the reset reason, the kind, IDF's reason or the abort text, the fault address, the task (`cli_console`: a typed command runs on the console's task), the uptime (against `/api/status` just before), the image id; its backtrace must be the frames the console's `Backtrace:` line delivered whole (at 2 Mbaud that line can break off: the note may hold more, never different ones), and decoded with the build's ELF it must name the function that crashed (`cmd_restart_tracker`, `panic_fault`, `panic_wdt`). **Flash**: a crash that is news is stored in NVS by the boot that files it, and the boot's own `WICAN FLASH` line must show that one small write (measured 9 to 12 writes, 468 to 500 bytes, 0 erases) and name it once (`crash report stored`); the SAME crash again (the second `--panic=fault`) must leave `WICAN FLASH writes=0 wbytes=0 erases=0 ebytes=0`; so must a planned restart. Every run that follows a crash is marked settled (`restart_tracker --settle`), so the four crashes are four first crashes and the brake stays out of this stage. `c8` power-cycles the DUT through the PSU: counters 0, no note out of power-on memory, and **the stored report still there, byte for byte, read back with `writes=0`**; `c9` clears it (`DELETE /api/restart/report`), settings untouched, no fault. `--no-power-cycle` on the script keeps notes and report for a look by hand. **Stage 2, the crash-loop brake and the crash park** (`crash_park_bench.py` → `CRASH PARK PASS`, 39 checks, ~5 min, 4 crashes + a forced sleep + a PSU power cycle): `p0` the two references: the supply current awake and in the firmware's own sleep mode (`sleep test 30`), and the LED chip breathing red on request, read back from its registers (`led -d`: `PATST=0x01`, `LCFG0=0x13`, `L0T0=0x61`, `L0T1=0x68`; the one part of a park no instrument on this rig can see); `p1` two quick `--panic=fault` with no settle in between: the DUT boots, the streak reads 1, then 2; `p2` the third: **parked** (console `WICAN PARK streak=3 parks=1 retry_s=40 bare=0`, no LED or OBD chip complaint, HTTP silent, supply current within 8 mA of sleep mode and under half of awake: measured 46 to 47 mA parked, 43 to 44 mA sleep mode, 158 to 164 mA awake); `p3` the bench's one-shot knob (`restart_tracker --park-retry 40`) ends the park after 41 s: one normal start, `park_retry` / `park` in the history, the record before it `mode: park`, the streak still 3, the stored report says `Loop: 3 crashes in a row; the device parked itself`, the whole loop cost exactly two flash writes (budget 4 → 2), and the awake current is back (USB rail, OBD chip); `p4` that start crashes too: parked AT ONCE (`streak=4 parks=2 retry_s=0`), and with no timer it stays parked (50 s watched) and writes nothing; `p5` a PSU power cycle is a fresh start: boot 1, the count empty, the report still there; `p6` as found. **Not in here, because it needs a hand on the device**: the button on a parked DUT (hold until sky blue = one start; keep holding until yellow = safe mode) and safe mode's crash report page; `TASK_crash_loop_brake.md` section 7 has the three-minute check by hand. A failed stage 2 power-cycles the DUT itself rather than leave it parked | DUT console + PSU + `ssh rpi001` |

Leg M: `22 F1 90` -> START_OF_MESSAGE (0x02, data `00 00 07 E8`) then the 17-byte VIN behind `62 F1 90`, in that order. Leg P: tx_flags 0 -> `7E0 dlc 3 [02 10 03]` on the bus and still answered, pad again -> dlc 8. Leg E: a second FLOW_CONTROL filter 7E1/7E9 on the same channel is answered from 7E9 while 7E0 still works, a 20-byte request to an id without a session is `ERR_NO_FLOW_CONTROL`, STOP_FILTER closes 7E1's session. Leg F (the filters cleared first, a flow-control session passes its id's frames as well): `[00 00 07 DF][01 00]` goes out as one frame and nothing is delivered; with a PASS filter on 7E8 the answer arrives as `[00 00 07 E8][41 00 ..]`. Leg X: settings as found (one restart), planned restarts only, no new E lines. | `python tools/testbench/usb/j2534_tp20_responder.py --blocks 240 --t3-ms 1` (PC, PCAN) in one window, then `C:\Users\Ali\idf_tmp\py32\python.exe -I tools/testbench/usb/j2534_tp20_tester.py --blocks 240 --batch 1` with `WICAN_J2534_HOST=192.168.82.1` (USB-NCM), `WICAN_J2534_COM=auto` (USB CDC serial, env `-u WICAN_J2534_HOST`) or the tunnel's `127.0.0.1:18534` (WiFi), `WICAN_J2534_DLL=<path>` for a built DLL | **The speed a TP 2.0 tool gets through the DLL** (prints `TESTER DONE` + `RESPONDER DONE` with bytes/s, the per-block time, the block-end ACK latency the ECU sees and the projected time of a 2 MB read; `TESTER FAIL` / `RESPONDER FAIL` on a lost frame or a missing ACK; ~16 s for 240 blocks; the second half of the 2026-10-27 report: "usually 20 min to flash, with wican 50"). The responder plays the ECU on PCAN: 255-byte blocks as 37 frames, `0x2N` for ordinary frames, `0x0N` at every 15th (block end: it waits for the tool's ACK `0xBN` before sending more), `0x1N` last; the tester (the DLL, 32-bit) requests blocks, answers every block end, reads one message per `PassThruReadMsgs` (`--batch 16` shows what a tool that asks for 16 pays: the DLL waited for all 16, 19 ms per ACK). Every block end is a round trip bus -> device -> DLL -> tool -> DLL -> device -> bus, and `PassThruWriteMsgs` blocks for the device's ACK (sent after the frame is on the bus): that round trip, not the bus, sets the clock. Measured 2026-10-27 (T3 1 ms, DLL 1.2): block-end ACK latency / per block / 2 MB projection = USB CDC serial 2.8 ms / 70 ms / 9.0 min, USB-NCM 4.7 ms / 76 ms / 9.8 min, WiFi station through the bench AP and the Pi 28.5 ms (p95 49, max 193) / 154 ms / 22.3 min; the real ECU's frame gap adds to all three. DLL 1.4 (`PassThruReadMsgs` on an event instead of a `Sleep(1)` poll, which costs a Windows timer tick: 15.6 ms unless an app raised the resolution, 1.3 to 1.9 ms on this PC) over USB-NCM: 4.4 ms / 56 ms / 7.7 min. 320 clean blocks over CDC after one unexplained `poweron` reboot of the DUT in the first CDC run (PSU steady at 13.5 V / 0.09 A through the reruns) | PCAN on the DUT bus; `j2534_server.enabled`; `usb_host_manager.role=device` with `device_class` ncm or cdc for the USB legs |
